===== LIB STATUS =====
     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * apriori status — answer "where am I / what's left" for a change.
     4	 * Reads the flow-state file + the issue ledger; zero deps, pure Node stdlib.
     5	 */
     6	const fs = require('fs');
     7	const path = require('path');
     8	const { withStrict } = require('./args');
     9	const { resolveChange, fileReadDefect, validateChangeName } = require('./resolve');
    10	
    11	// parse `key: value` lines from a flow-state file → object; plus the last gate line
    12	function parseFlowState(text) {
    13	  const out = {};
    14	  for (const key of ['change', 'tier', 'track', 'current-step', 'round', 'next-action', 'lineage']) {
    15	    const m = text.match(new RegExp('^' + key + ':\\s*(.+)$', 'm'));
    16	    if (m) out[key] = m[1].replace(/\s*#.*$/, '').trim();
    17	  }
    18	  const gates = [...text.matchAll(/^\s*-\s*(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}\S*\s+.+)$/gm)].map((m) => m[1].trim());
    19	  out.lastGate = gates.length ? gates[gates.length - 1] : null;
    20	  return out;
    21	}
    22	
    23	// parse a P0 ledger markdown table → rows with {id, status}
    24	function parseLedger(text) {
    25	  const rows = [];
    26	  for (const line of text.split('\n')) {
    27	    const m = line.match(/^\|\s*([^|]+?)\s*\|.*\|\s*([^|]+?)\s*\|\s*$/);
    28	    if (!m) continue;
    29	    const id = m[1].trim(), status = m[2].trim();
    30	    if (id === 'ID' || /^-+$/.test(id)) continue;         // header / separator
    31	    rows.push({ id, status });
    32	  }
    33	  return rows;
    34	}
    35	
    36	function activeChanges(root) {
    37	  const dir = path.join(root, 'apriori', 'changes');
    38	  if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) return [];
    39	  return fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })
    40	    .filter((e) => e.isDirectory() && e.name !== 'archive')
    41	    .map((e) => e.name).sort();
    42	}
    43	
    44	// dir defaults to the active path (unit-level compat); cli passes the resolved bundle
    45	function changeStatus(root, change, dir, stage) {
    46	  const bundle = dir || path.join(root, 'apriori', 'changes', change);
    47	  const fsPath = path.join(bundle, 'flow-state.md');
    48	  const flowSafe = fileReadDefect(bundle, fsPath) === null;
    49	  const state = flowSafe ? parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(fsPath, 'utf8')) : null;
    50	  const ledgerPath = path.join(bundle, 'review', 'issues.md');
    51	  const ledgerSafe = fileReadDefect(bundle, ledgerPath) === null;
    52	  const rows = ledgerSafe ? parseLedger(fs.readFileSync(ledgerPath, 'utf8')) : [];
    53	  const open = rows.filter((r) => /^open\b/i.test(r.status));
    54	  return { change, state, open, hasFlowState: !!state, stage: stage || 'in-flight', path: path.relative(root, bundle) };
    55	}
    56	
    57	// cli --change guards: name validation + resolver + structured file-level defects (kinds,
    58	// never message prefixes); the optional ledger is benign ONLY on kind 'missing'
    59	function guardedResolve(root, change) {
    60	  const v = validateChangeName(change);
    61	  if (!v.ok) {
    62	    const why = { 'invalid-shape': 'bare kebab-case only', 'date-prefixed': 'date-prefixed — in-flight names are bare; dates are stamped at archive time', reserved: `'${change}' is reserved` }[v.kind];
    63	    return { error: `invalid change name '${change}' — ${why}` };
    64	  }
    65	  const loc = resolveChange(root, change);
    66	  if (loc.error) return { error: loc.error };
    67	  const flowDefect = fileReadDefect(loc.dir, path.join(loc.dir, 'flow-state.md'));
    68	  if (flowDefect) return { error: `flow-state.md unreadable for '${change}' — ${flowDefect.kind}: ${flowDefect.path}` };
    69	  const ledgerDefect = fileReadDefect(loc.dir, path.join(loc.dir, 'review', 'issues.md'));
    70	  if (ledgerDefect && ledgerDefect.kind !== 'missing') return { error: `review/issues.md unsafe for '${change}' — ${ledgerDefect.kind}: ${ledgerDefect.path}` };
    71	  // identity: the parsed flow-state must speak the queried name (P2-1)
    72	  const state = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(path.join(loc.dir, 'flow-state.md'), 'utf8'));
    73	  if (state.change && state.change !== change) return { error: `identity mismatch for '${change}' — the resolved flow-state declares change: ${state.change}` };
    74	  return loc;
    75	}
    76	
    77	function formatOne(s) {
    78	  const lines = [];
    79	  if (!s.state) { lines.push(`change: ${s.change} (no flow-state file found)`); }
    80	  else {
    81	    lines.push(`change:       ${s.state.change || s.change}` + (s.stage === 'archived' ? '   (archived)' : ''));
    82	    lines.push(`step:         ${s.state['current-step'] || '?'}   (tier ${s.state.tier || '?'}, track ${s.state.track || '?'})`);
    83	    if (s.state['next-action']) lines.push(`next-action:  ${s.state['next-action']}`);
    84	    if (s.state.lastGate) lines.push(`last gate:    ${s.state.lastGate}`);
    85	  }
    86	  lines.push(`open ledger:  ${s.open.length}` + (s.open.length ? ` — ${s.open.map((r) => r.id).join(', ')}` : ''));
    87	  return lines.join('\n');
    88	}
    89	
    90	// machine-consumable shape for one change
    91	function toJson(s) {
    92	  return {
    93	    change: s.change,
    94	    step: s.state ? s.state['current-step'] || null : null,
    95	    tier: s.state ? s.state.tier || null : null,
    96	    track: s.state ? s.state.track || null : null,
    97	    lineage: s.state ? s.state.lineage || null : null,
    98	    nextAction: s.state ? s.state['next-action'] || null : null,
    99	    lastGate: s.state ? s.state.lastGate : null,
   100	    hasFlowState: s.hasFlowState,
   101	    openLedger: s.open.map((r) => r.id),
   102	    stage: s.stage,
   103	    path: s.path,
   104	  };
   105	}
   106	
   107	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori status [--change <name>] [--json]';
   108	
   109	function cli(argv) {
   110	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'status', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   111	    flags: { '--change': 'value', '--json': 'flag' } }, (f) => {
   112	    const root = process.cwd();
   113	    const change = f['--change'] || null, json = !!f['--json'];
   114	    if (change) {
   115	      const loc = guardedResolve(root, change);
   116	      if (loc.error) { console.error(`status: ${loc.error}`); return 2; }
   117	      const s = changeStatus(root, change, loc.dir, loc.stage);
   118	      console.log(json ? JSON.stringify(toJson(s), null, 2) : formatOne(s));
   119	      return 0;
   120	    }
   121	    const changes = activeChanges(root);
   122	    if (json) {
   123	      console.log(JSON.stringify({ changes: changes.map((c) => toJson(changeStatus(root, c))) }, null, 2));
   124	      return 0;
   125	    }
   126	    if (!changes.length) { console.log('No active changes under apriori/changes/.'); return 0; }
   127	    console.log(`Active changes (${changes.length}):\n`);
   128	    for (const c of changes) {
   129	      const s = changeStatus(root, c);
   130	      const step = s.state ? s.state['current-step'] || '?' : 'no flow-state';
   131	      console.log(`  ${c}  —  ${step}, ${s.open.length} open`);
   132	    }
   133	    console.log('\nRun `apriori status --change <name>` for detail.');
   134	    return 0;
   135	  });
   136	}
   137	
   138	module.exports = { parseFlowState, parseLedger, activeChanges, changeStatus, formatOne, toJson, cli };
===== LIB NEW =====
     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * apriori new — scaffold an in-flight change directory with a flow-state skeleton.
     4	 * Zero deps — pure Node stdlib. In-flight names are BARE (no date prefix, §4);
     5	 * archive stamps dates at archive time.
     6	 */
     7	const fs = require('fs');
     8	const path = require('path');
     9	const { withStrict } = require('./args');
    10	
    11	const NAME_RE = /^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$/;   // bare kebab-case: no empty segments, no trailing hyphen
    12	
    13	function flowStateSkeleton(name, now) {
    14	  const p = (n) => String(n).padStart(2, '0');
    15	  const stamp = `${now.getFullYear()}-${p(now.getMonth() + 1)}-${p(now.getDate())}T${p(now.getHours())}:${p(now.getMinutes())}`;
    16	  return `change: ${name}
    17	tier: <trivial | medium | large>          # size it per runbook §2
    18	track: <harden | explore>                 # certainty axis, §2 (unsure: harden)
    19	track-rationale: <one line — reported at the next human gate>
    20	lineage: <target branch/line + merge taboo — copy from the requirement>
    21	current-step: STEP0
    22	round: 0
    23	reviewer-session: n/a                     # record the reviewer's resumable session id the moment round 1 prints it (R2)
    24	next-action: draft apriori/changes/${name}/requirement/req-v1.md (or the intent card on the explore track)
    25	artifact-root: .                          # optional; root for process artifacts (runbook §3) — default project root
    26	gates:
    27	  - ${stamp} note: change scaffolded by \`apriori new\`
    28	`;
    29	}
    30	
    31	// Scaffold apriori/changes/<name>/flow-state.md. Returns {ok, path?|error?}.
    32	function scaffoldChange(root, name, now) {
    33	  const v = require('./resolve').validateChangeName(name);   // single source of name truth
    34	  if (!v.ok) {
    35	    if (v.kind === 'invalid-shape') return { ok: false, error: `invalid change name '${name}' — use bare kebab-case (e.g. add-playback); dates are stamped at archive time, not here` };
    36	    if (v.kind === 'date-prefixed') return { ok: false, error: `'${name}' looks date-prefixed — in-flight names are bare; dates are stamped at archive time, not here` };
    37	    return { ok: false, error: `'archive' is reserved for apriori/changes/archive/` };
    38	  }
    39	  const dir = path.join(root, 'apriori', 'changes', name);
    40	  if (fs.existsSync(dir)) return { ok: false, error: `change '${name}' already exists at ${path.relative(root, dir)}` };
    41	  // the bundle skeleton: specs/ for deltas, requirement/ for the req history, review/ for evidence
    42	  for (const sub of ['specs', 'requirement', 'review']) fs.mkdirSync(path.join(dir, sub), { recursive: true });
    43	  const flow = path.join(dir, 'flow-state.md');
    44	  fs.writeFileSync(flow, flowStateSkeleton(name, now));
    45	  return { ok: true, path: path.relative(root, flow) };
    46	}
    47	
    48	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori new <change-name>   (bare kebab-case, e.g. add-playback)';
    49	
    50	function cli(argv) {
    51	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'new', usage: USAGE, positionals: 1, flags: {} }, (f, pos) => {
    52	    const name = pos[0];
    53	    const r = scaffoldChange(process.cwd(), name, new Date());
    54	    if (!r.ok) { console.error(`  ✗ ${r.error}`); return 1; }
    55	    console.log(`  ✓ ${r.path}`);
    56	    console.log(`\n  Next: fill in tier/track/lineage, then kick off:\n    /apriori ${name}   (or the runbook §0 kickoff prompt)`);
    57	    return 0;
    58	  });
    59	}
    60	
    61	module.exports = { NAME_RE, flowStateSkeleton, scaffoldChange, cli };

652:// High-level archive: the whole change, four phases (preflight → stage → commit → move).

     9	
    10	  new       scaffold a change dir + flow-state skeleton (bare kebab-case name)

   623	// ---- archive commit machinery ----------------------------------------------
   624	
   625	// colon-free date-time stamp YYYY-MM-DDThhmm (CLI's own clock; never an agent guess)
   626	function archiveStamp(d) {
   627	  const p = (n) => String(n).padStart(2, '0');
   628	  return `${d.getFullYear()}-${p(d.getMonth() + 1)}-${p(d.getDate())}T${p(d.getHours())}${p(d.getMinutes())}`;
   629	}
   630	
   631	const CHANGE_NAME_RE = /^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$/;   // bare kebab-case — same rule as `apriori new`
   632	
   633	
   634	// Move an in-flight change dir (bare name) → changes/archive/<stamp>-<name>/
   635	// Fail-closed: source AND destination must resolve (realpath) strictly inside changesDir.
   636	function archiveChangeDir(changesDir, name, now, ops) {
   637	  const rename = (ops && ops.renameSync) || fs.renameSync;
   638	  if (!CHANGE_NAME_RE.test(name)) throw new Error(`invalid change name '${name}' — bare kebab-case only`);
   639	  const src = path.join(changesDir, name);
   640	  if (!fs.existsSync(src)) return null;
   641	  if (!containsReal(changesDir, src)) throw new Error(`change path escapes ${changesDir}`);
   642	  const dest = path.join(changesDir, 'archive', `${archiveStamp(now)}-${name}`);
   643	  if (!containsReal(changesDir, dest)) throw new Error(`archive destination escapes ${changesDir} (symlinked archive/?)`);
   644	  fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(dest), { recursive: true });
   645	  rename(src, dest);
   646	  return dest;
   647	}
   648	
   649	const CATEGORY_LABELS = [['merged (ADDED)', 'merged'], ['modified (MODIFIED)', 'modified'],
   650	  ['deprecated (REMOVED)', 'deprecated'], ['renamed (RENAMED)', 'renamed'], ['already merged (no-op)', 'unchanged']];
   651	
   652	// High-level archive: the whole change, four phases (preflight → stage → commit → move).
   653	// → { code, out: [], err: [] } — pure return; cli() prints. ops is the fault-injection seam.
   654	function archiveChange(o) {
   655	  const out = [], err = [];
   656	  const ops = o.ops || { writeFileSync: fs.writeFileSync.bind(fs), renameSync: fs.renameSync.bind(fs), rmSync: fs.rmSync.bind(fs) };
   657	  const cwd = o.cwd || process.cwd();
   658	  const changesDir = path.resolve(cwd, o.changesDir || path.join('apriori', 'changes'));
   659	  const storeRoot = path.resolve(cwd, 'apriori', 'specs');
   660	
   661	  // --- phase 1: preflight (no writes) ---
   662	  const d = discoverDeltas(changesDir, o.change);
   663	  if (d.errors.length) { err.push(...d.errors.map((e) => `archive: ${e}`)); return { code: 2, out, err }; }
   664	  const p = buildProjection(storeRoot, d.files, o.change);
   665	  if (p.validation.length) { err.push(...p.validation.map((e) => `archive: ${e}`)); return { code: 2, out, err }; }
   666	  // per-module report for whatever merged cleanly (dry-run info comes first, then failures)
   667	  for (const suffix of p.modules) {
   668	    const r = p.perModule.get(suffix);
   669	    if (!r || r.conflicts.length) continue;
   670	    out.push(`--- ${suffix}`);
   671	    for (const [label, key] of CATEGORY_LABELS) if (r[key].length) out.push(`${label}: ${r[key].join(', ')}`);
   672	  }
   673	  const casDenials = [];
   674	  if (p.unstampedMutations.length) {
   675	    const w = casWaiver(cwd, o.noCas);
   676	    if (w.problem) {
   677	      casDenials.push(`${w.problem} — a broken config never equals a waiver (denied)`);
   678	    } else if (!w.waiver) {
   679	      for (const sfx of p.unstampedMutations)
   680	        casDenials.push(`${unstampedWarning(sfx)} — denied by default; waive with --no-cas or a | cas | optional | config row`);
   681	    } else {
   682	      for (const sfx of p.unstampedMutations) out.push(`warning: ${unstampedWarning(sfx)}`);
   683	      out.push(`note: cas waived by ${w.waiver}`);
   684	    }
   685	  }
   686	  for (const n of p.notes) out.push(`note: ${n}`);
   687	  // CAS denial joins the preflight failure set — every diagnosis stays visible in one report
   688	  const preflightFailures = [...casDenials, ...p.hygiene, ...p.casMismatches, ...p.conflicts];
   689	  if (preflightFailures.length) {
   690	    for (const f of preflightFailures) err.push(`archive: ${f}`);
   691	    out.push('\nRESULT: FAILED PREFLIGHT — nothing written');
   692	    return { code: 1, out, err };
   693	  }
   694	  const repaired = new Set(p.repaired);                     // already applied — no writes for these
   695	  const jobs = d.files.filter(({ suffix }) => !repaired.has(suffix)).map(({ suffix }) => {
   696	    const store = path.join(storeRoot, ...suffix.split('/'));
   697	    return { suffix, store, tmp: store + '.tmp-archive', text: p.texts.get(suffix) };
   698	  }).sort((a, b) => (a.store < b.store ? -1 : 1));
   699	  if (o.write) {
   700	    for (const j of jobs) if (fs.existsSync(j.tmp)) {
   701	      err.push(`archive: temp file already exists: ${j.tmp} — another run in flight or a manual-recovery artifact; not touching it`);
   702	      out.push('\nRESULT: FAILED PREFLIGHT — nothing written');
   703	      return { code: 1, out, err };
   704	    }
   705	    if (o.changesDirExplicit) {
   706	      // destination containment is checked before any write, not at move time
   707	      const probe = path.join(changesDir, 'archive', 'probe');
   708	      if (!containsReal(changesDir, probe)) {
   709	        err.push(`archive: archive destination escapes ${changesDir} (symlinked archive/?)`);
   710	        return { code: 2, out, err };
   711	      }
   712	    }
   713	  }
   714	  // the modified-block integrity section: AFTER every preflight guard (incl. the --write
   715	  // temp/containment checks above), BEFORE any write-result line — informative only
   716	  pushIntegritySection(out, err, p.modifiedBlocks, o.idMatcherFactory, cwd);
   717	  if (!o.write) {
   718	    out.push(`\nRESULT: MERGED (dry-run; ${jobs.length} module(s)) — pass --write to apply`);
   719	    return { code: 0, out, err };
   720	  }
   721	
   722	  // --- phase 2: stage (this run's temps only — preflight proved none pre-existed) ---
   723	  const staged = [];
   724	  try {
   725	    for (const j of jobs) {
   726	      fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(j.store), { recursive: true });
   727	      ops.writeFileSync(j.tmp, j.text);
   728	      staged.push(j.tmp);
   729	    }
   730	  } catch (e) {
   731	    for (const t of staged) { try { ops.rmSync(t, { force: true }); } catch { /* best effort */ } }
   732	    err.push(`archive: staging failed (${e.message}) — stores untouched, this run's temp files removed`);
   733	    return { code: 1, out, err };
   734	  }
   735	
   736	  // --- phase 3: commit (per-file atomic renames, sorted order; no rollback on mid-commit failure) ---
   737	  const committed = [];
   738	  for (const j of jobs) {
   739	    try { ops.renameSync(j.tmp, j.store); committed.push(j); }
   740	    catch (e) {
   741	      err.push(`archive: COMMIT FAILED at ${j.suffix} (${e.message})`);
   742	      err.push(`  committed:     ${committed.map((c) => c.suffix).join(', ') || '(none)'}`);
   743	      const remaining = jobs.filter((x) => !committed.includes(x));
   744	      err.push(`  NOT committed: ${remaining.map((x) => x.suffix).join(', ')}`);
   745	      err.push(`  temp files remaining for manual completion: ${remaining.map((x) => x.tmp).join(', ')}`);
   746	      return { code: 1, out, err };
   747	    }
   748	  }
   749	
   750	  // --- phase 4: move (only with explicit --changes-dir) ---
   751	  let moved = null;
   752	  if (o.changesDirExplicit) {
   753	    try { moved = archiveChangeDir(changesDir, o.change, new Date(), ops); }
   754	    catch (e) { err.push(`archive: stores committed but the change-dir move failed: ${e.message} — rerun to complete`); return { code: 1, out, err }; }
   755	    if (moved === null) { err.push('archive: stores committed but the change dir vanished before the move'); return { code: 1, out, err }; }
   756	  }
   757	  out.push(`\nRESULT: MERGED — ${jobs.length} module store(s) rewritten${moved ? `; change archived → ${moved}` : ''}`);
   758	  return { code: 0, out, err };
   759	}
   760	
   761	// print the CAS stamp line for a store file's current content
   762	const STAMP_USAGE = 'usage: apriori stamp <store-file>';
   763	function stampCli(argv) {
   764	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'stamp', usage: STAMP_USAGE, positionals: 1, flags: {} }, (f, pos) => {
   765	    const p = pos[0];
   766	    if (!fs.existsSync(p)) { console.log('<!-- apriori-base: new -->'); return 0; }
   767	    if (fs.statSync(p).isDirectory()) { console.error(`stamp: ${p} is a directory, not a store file`); return 2; }
   768	    let text;
   769	    try { text = fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8'); }
   770	    catch (e) { console.error(`stamp: cannot read ${p}: ${e.message}`); return 2; }
   771	    console.log(`<!-- apriori-base: ${fingerprint(text)} -->`);
   772	    return 0;
   773	  });
   774	}
   775	
   776	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori archive --store <f> --delta <f> --change <name> [--write] [--changes-dir <dir>] [--no-cas]\n   or: apriori archive --change <name> [--write] [--changes-dir <dir>] [--no-cas]';
   777	
   778	function cli(argv, deps = {}) {
   779	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'archive', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   780	    flags: { '--store': 'value', '--delta': 'value', '--change': 'value', '--write': 'flag', '--changes-dir': 'value', '--no-cas': 'flag' } }, (f) => {
===== ARCHIVE SINGLE FILE =====
   780	    flags: { '--store': 'value', '--delta': 'value', '--change': 'value', '--write': 'flag', '--changes-dir': 'value', '--no-cas': 'flag' } }, (f) => {
   781	    const a = { store: f['--store'] || null, delta: f['--delta'] || null, change: f['--change'] || null,
   782	      write: !!f['--write'], changesDir: f['--changes-dir'] || null, noCas: !!f['--no-cas'] };
   783	  // high-level form: --change alone (no --store/--delta) drives the whole change
   784	    if (a.change && !a.store && !a.delta) {
   785	      const r = archiveChange({ cwd: process.cwd(), change: a.change, write: a.write,
   786	        changesDir: a.changesDir || undefined, changesDirExplicit: !!a.changesDir, noCas: a.noCas,
   787	        idMatcherFactory: deps.idMatcherFactory });
   788	      for (const l of r.out) console.log(l);
   789	      for (const l of r.err) console.error(l);
   790	      return r.code;
   791	    }
   792	    if (a.change && (a.store || a.delta) && !(a.store && a.delta)) {
   793	      console.error('usage: apriori archive --change <name> [--write] [--changes-dir <dir>]  (high-level form)\n   or: apriori archive --store <f> --delta <f> --change <name> [--write] [--changes-dir <dir>]  (single-file form)\n(--change cannot be combined with only one of --store/--delta)');
   794	      return 2;
   795	    }
   796	    if (!a.store || !a.delta || !a.change) { console.error(USAGE); return 2; }
   797	    const vEarly = require('./resolve').validateChangeName(a.change);
   798	    if (!vEarly.ok) { console.error(`archive: invalid change name '${a.change}' (${vEarly.kind}) — bare kebab-case, not date-prefixed, not a reserved name`); return 2; }
   799	    if (a.changesDir && !fs.existsSync(path.join(a.changesDir, a.change))) {
   800	      console.error(`archive: change '${a.change}' not found under ${a.changesDir} — nothing to move`); return 2;
   801	    }
   802	    // first archive in a repo: the store file may not exist yet — start from an empty store
   803	    const storeExists = fs.existsSync(a.store);
   804	    const storeText = storeExists ? fs.readFileSync(a.store, 'utf8') : '';
   805	    if (!storeExists) console.log(`store ${a.store} does not exist yet — will be created${a.write ? '' : ' on --write'}`);
   806	    const deltaText = fs.readFileSync(a.delta, 'utf8');
   807	    const { delta, stamp, problems } = parseDeltaStrict(deltaText);
   808	    if (problems.length) {
   809	      for (const p of problems) console.error(`archive: ${a.delta}: ${p}`);
   810	      console.log('\nRESULT: MALFORMED DELTA — nothing written');
   811	      return 1;
   812	    }
   813	    // Guard against a silent no-op: a delta with real content that parsed to zero operations
   814	    // is almost always a format mismatch (wrong heading level/keyword), not an empty change.
   815	    if (deltaText.trim() && deltaOpCount(delta) === 0) {
   816	      console.error(`archive: parsed 0 delta operations from ${a.delta} — it has content but no recognized operations.`);
   817	      console.error('  expected `## ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED Requirements` (h2) with `### Requirement: <name>` (h3) blocks,');
   818	      console.error('  or `## RENAMED Requirements` with `- Old -> New` lines. Nothing written.');
   819	      return 1;
   820	    }
   821	    // AM-32 (4.0): mutation ops without a stamp are denied unless visibly waived
   822	    if (!stamp && mutationOpCount(delta) > 0) {
   823	      const w = casWaiver(process.cwd(), a.noCas);
   824	      if (w.problem || !w.waiver) {
   825	        console.error(`archive: ${w.problem ? w.problem + ' — a broken config never equals a waiver (denied)' : unstampedWarning(a.delta) + ' — denied by default; waive with --no-cas or a | cas | optional | config row'}`);
   826	        console.log('\nRESULT: FAILED PREFLIGHT — nothing written');
   827	        return 1;
   828	      }
   829	      console.log(`warning: ${unstampedWarning(a.delta)}`);
   830	      console.log(`note: cas waived by ${w.waiver}`);
   831	    }
   832	    // CAS: a present base stamp must match the store the delta is about to merge into (AM-24/AM-26)
   833	    if (stamp) {
   834	      const actual = storeExists ? fingerprint(storeText) : 'new';
   835	      if (stamp !== actual) {
   836	        console.error(`archive: base mismatch for ${a.store}: delta expects ${stamp}, store is ${actual} — nothing written (serialize per §4.11)`);
   837	        return 1;
   838	      }
   839	    }
   840	    const { store, merged, modified, deprecated, renamed, conflicts, unchanged } = merge(storeText, delta, a.change);
   841	
   842	    for (const [label, ids] of [['merged (ADDED)', merged], ['modified (MODIFIED)', modified], ['deprecated (REMOVED)', deprecated], ['renamed (RENAMED)', renamed], ['already merged (no-op)', unchanged]])
   843	      if (ids.length) console.log(`${label}: ${ids.join(', ')}`);
   844	
   845	    if (conflicts.length) {
   846	      console.log('\nCONFLICTS (stop — open a ledger issue, human resolves):');
   847	      for (const c of conflicts) console.log(`  ✗ ${c}`);
   848	      console.log('\nRESULT: CONFLICT — nothing written');
   849	      return 1;
   850	    }
   851	    if (a.write) {
   852	      // Transactional ordering: stage the store to a temp file, do the (atomic) dir move, then
   853	      // commit the store via rename. If the move fails, the temp is discarded and the store on
   854	      // disk stays byte-for-byte untouched — never a half-completed archive.
   855	      fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(a.store), { recursive: true });
   856	      const tmp = a.store + '.tmp-archive';
   857	      fs.writeFileSync(tmp, renderStore(storeText, store));
   858	      let moved = null;
   859	      if (a.changesDir) {
   860	        try { moved = archiveChangeDir(a.changesDir, a.change, new Date()); }
   861	        catch (e) { fs.rmSync(tmp, { force: true }); console.error(`archive: ${e.message} — store left untouched`); return 1; }
   862	        if (moved === null) { fs.rmSync(tmp, { force: true }); console.error(`archive: change dir vanished mid-run — store left untouched`); return 1; }
   863	      }
   864	      fs.renameSync(tmp, a.store);
   865	      console.log(`\nRESULT: MERGED — ${a.store} rewritten${moved ? `; change archived → ${moved}` : ''}`);
   866	    } else {
   867	      console.log(`\nRESULT: MERGED (dry-run; ${store.size} requirements in result) — pass --write to apply`);
   868	    }
   869	    return 0;
   870	  });
   871	}
   872	module.exports = { parseRequirements, parseRequirementsStrict, parseDelta, parseDeltaStrict, deltaOpCount, mutationOpCount, unstampedWarning,
   873	  compareModifiedBlock, formatIntegrityHuman,
   874	  merge, renderStore, archiveStamp, archiveChangeDir, archiveChange, CHANGE_NAME_RE,
   875	  fingerprint, parseStamp, containsReal, discoverDeltas, buildProjection,
===== RUNBOOK STEP6/P0 =====
   375	```text
   376	A different model reviewed your spec and design: apriori/changes/<change>/review/spec-review-v{N}.md.
   377	Handle each formal item (accept/reject + reason), modifying spec and design files only — never source.
   378	Advisories may be batch-acknowledged or ignored without per-item reasons — only rejections of formal findings need justification.
   379	Update each issue's Status in the ledger, then request review round v{N+1}.
   380	```
   381	
   382	### P7 — STEP5 apply (producer)
   383	
   384	```text
   385	Tests first: derive one failing test per spec scenario, named with its scenario ID (e.g. test('KV-03 …')), and show the failing run.
   386	Then implement strictly in tasks.md order; mark each task [x] immediately on completion.
   387	* Scenario coverage is the hard bar: every scenario has ≥1 test carrying its ID. Line coverage is a signal, never a target — no assertion-free padding;
   388	* Log at key branches and function entries per the project convention;
   389	* Before declaring green, run the project's linter/static analysis (where configured);
   390	* For any continue/skip/silently-ignored branch, re-check the spec for required user-visibility.
   391	* UI projects: don't fly blind — while implementing, render what you built and LOOK at it
   392	  (e.g. Playwright screenshots of the running page, simulated clicks along the core flows).
   393	  Screenshots go to `apriori/tmp/` (gitignored — they are instruments, never committed artifacts);
   394	  what persists is your one-line textual observation of what the screenshot showed.
   395	  Drive the SPEC BOUNDARIES, not just the happy path: every range the spec states (min AND max —
   396	  e.g. a 2..20-option form must let you actually build a 20-option poll) and every rejection the spec
   397	  requires must be REACHABLE and exercised through the real UI. A UI that silently can't reach a spec'd
   398	  path — a hard cap below the max, an input that pre-filters what the server is spec'd to reject — is a
   399	  spec-vs-code gap (P8 dimension 5's sibling): the front end must be able to produce every input the
   400	  backend spec promises to handle or reject, or the guarantee is fiction from the user's seat. When the
   401	  UI catches invalid input the server would reject, it must **surface the rejection to the user**, not
   402	  silently drop or swallow it — a discarded blank option the user typed is a hidden failure, not validation.
   403	(Docs-only projects: the "test suite" is `apriori check` — same failing-first discipline where feasible.)
   404	Run the tests until green; stop and wait for archive.
   405	```
   406	
   407	### P8 — STEP5 consistency reviewer (heterogeneous, R2)
   408	
   409	```text
   410	Review the implementation against the SPEC-DOC. `apriori verify` has already proven the mechanical
   411	binding (every scenario has a passing test, no orphans); your job is what binding cannot prove —
   412	whether each test faithfully exercises its scenario's INTENT:
   413	1. Semantic faithfulness: for each scenario, does its test actually assert the behavior the scenario
   414	   describes, or merely share its ID while asserting something weaker/nothing (a green test can be empty);
   415	2. Behavior the spec requires but the code doesn't implement (that a bound test failed to catch);
   416	3. continue/skip/silently-ignored branches — does the spec require them to be user-visible;
   417	4. Where external input or permissions are touched: unvalidated input, missing authz, secrets/PII in logs.
   418	5. Guarantee claims: every "always / under concurrency / crash-durable / persisted-on-success / atomic" phrase in the spec or KB must have a test that injects the adversarial condition and observes it hold — an unexercised hard guarantee is a spec-vs-code gap (§4.8 guarantee-claim discipline), not advisory.
   419	[Scope] Count toward the verdict only spec-vs-code gaps. Style, taste and nice-to-haves — label advisory (P0 rules). If you run tests yourself in a read-only sandbox, treat degraded output as a sandbox artifact, not a finding (R2).
   420	List each inconsistency with a suggested fix; end with your ledger delta (P0 rules), advisories listed separately.
   421	(Docs-only projects: read "tests" as the doc checks; `apriori check` stands in for the binding gate.)
   422	End with the verdict line (§5 phrase table): "VERDICT: no spec-vs-code gaps" or "VERDICT: <N> issues open".
   423	```
   424	
   425	### P9 — STEP6 archive (producer)
   426	
   427	```text
   428	Archive this change per the interface's archive algorithm (§4) — list every merged/modified/deprecated/renamed ID; on a same-ID conflict, stop and open a ledger issue. Then update the knowledge base in lockstep. KB docs have two sections with opposite truth directions:
   429	* "## Contract (code-is-truth)": update it from the final implementation; refresh the source-commit stamp (it covers this section only);
   430	* "## Decisions (doc-is-truth)": append decisions/invariants/rejected alternatives made in this change, each with status (active / superseded-by: <id>). NEVER rewrite an active invariant to match code — if the code violates one, file a bug instead;
   431	List exactly which KB files and sections you updated.
   432	```
   433	
   434	### P10 — KB reverse-capture / reconcile (legacy projects)
   435	
   436	```text
   437	You are a system knowledge-base engineer. Read the module's code and produce/reconcile its KB doc.
   438	[Input] Code scope: <dirs/files>. Existing KB (if any): apriori/truth/<module>.md
   439	(First contact with unfamiliar code and no module map? One KB doc for the whole app is fine up to
   440	a few kLOC; split along ownership seams — storage/domain/transport — only when a doc stops fitting
   441	one read. Capture records what IS: it is NOT a defect audit — do not promise found-bug coverage;
   442	commission an audit separately if wanted.)
   443	[Task] Abstract: public responsibilities/interfaces, core data flow, key state and side effects (the three moments), dependencies, conventions and pitfalls. If a KB exists, flag every mismatched/stale/missing point and revise — per the section rules below.
   444	[Output] apriori/truth/<module>.md on the change branch (so the PR diff is where it gets reviewed), structured as two fixed sections with opposite truth directions:
   445	* "## Contract (code-is-truth)" — interfaces, three moments, code-derived pitfalls; here code IS the sole source of truth: reconcile from it and stamp with the source-commit you read (the stamp covers this section only);
   446	* "## Decisions (doc-is-truth)" — decisions, invariants, rejected alternatives, each with status (active / superseded-by: <id>); NEVER reconcile this section from code — where code contradicts an active invariant, flag it as a bug in your output instead of editing the entry.
   447	[Constraints] Contract: only facts present in the code. Decisions: only explicitly confirmed intent. Mark uncertainties "needs human confirmation"; never invent abstract intent.
   448	```

   450	### P11 — explore track: spec extraction (producer)
   451	
   452	```text
   453	[Input] apriori/changes/<change>/requirement/intent-card.md; the prototype under changes/<change>/spike/; the spike findings.
   454	[Task] Extract the specification implied by the prototype's *validated* behaviors — never invent behavior that neither the intent card nor an observed spike run supports. Produce:
   455	* spec drafts with scenario IDs under apriori/changes/<change>/specs/ — the SOLE intent-side authority;
   456	* apriori/changes/<change>/requirement/req-final.md — a THIN INDEX only: one goal line citing the intent card + acceptance = a reference to the spec scenario-ID list. Never write a second acceptance narrative there — two prose versions of the same intent drift apart.
   457	[Constraints] Mark unvalidated assumptions "needs confirmation". Behavior that neither the intent card nor an observed spike run supports, but the spec needs for completeness, MUST be declared as an explicit extraction-time decision — an `EXT-n` entry (content + reasoning) in a dedicated section, never mixed into extracted facts; EXT-n entries are ruled on at the extraction review. The prototype is a source of observations, not of authority: where intent and prototype disagree, the intent card wins and the disagreement is listed explicitly.
   458	Stop and wait for the extraction review (P12).
   459	```

   461	### P12 — explore track: extraction review (heterogeneous, R2)
   462	
   463	```text
   464	[Input] apriori/changes/<change>/requirement/intent-card.md; P11's outputs; the issue ledger.
   465	[Checklist] P1's five dimensions, plus:
   466	6. Intent-card conformance — every goal and success criterion appears in the extracted specs/ (the sole authority; the req-final thin index is checked only for being thin and consistent);
   467	7. No invention — every spec line traces to the intent card or an observed spike behavior (spot-check the tracing), EXCEPT declared EXT-n entries, which are reviewed as proposals: recommend each as accepted / rejected / needs-human.
   468	[EXT-n semantics] Your verdict line judges extraction faithfulness only (invention outside declared EXT-n, intent conformance) — EXT-n recommendations never change it. Final EXT-n rulings belong to the `extraction review` decision point (the existing human gate): human-rejected → the producer deletes those spec lines, deletion confirmed mechanically (grep: the EXT-n scenario IDs are gone) with no P12 rerun; human-accepted → the entry is back-noted on the intent card. Unruled EXT-n block the decision point, not your verdict line — list them explicitly before it.
   469	[Scope] Count toward the verdict only unfaithful extraction or a falsified intent hypothesis; advisory findings never land in either rejected branch (P0 rules).
   470	[Output] apriori/changes/<change>/review/extraction-review-v{N}.md — issues per P0, advisories listed separately, EXT-n recommendations; end with your ledger delta,

RUNBOOK.md:76:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/changes/<change>/review/<stem>-raw.* (the stem = its review doc)` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source; when landing a raw, prepend the one-line provenance header `<!-- provenance: provider=<name> model=<id> session=<id> date=<YYYY-MM-DD> -->` (unknown fields written `unknown`; older raws are grandfathered). The same transcription mechanism covers the **review doc itself**: a read-only reviewer prints the doc body to stdout, and the producer lands it verbatim at its fixed path — that is the intended flow, not a workaround. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` (PowerShell has no /dev/null: pipe instead, `$null | codex exec …`) — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. For this to survive a *producer*-side interruption too, record the reviewer's session id in flow-state's `reviewer-session` field the moment round 1 prints it — otherwise a resume after a crash has no session to reattach to. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.

   202	  const stripFencesKb = (t) => t.replace(/```[\s\S]*?```/g, '');
   203	  const blocked = [], notes = [];
   204	  let checked = 0;
   205	  for (const m of [...modules].sort()) {
   206	    if (conflicts.has(m)) { blocked.push(conflicts.get(m)); continue; }
   207	    const entry = index.get(m);
   208	    if (!entry) { notes.push(`${m}: no truth doc`); continue; }
   209	    const noFence = stripFencesKb(entry.text);
   210	    const sc = noFence.match(/^source-commit:\s+(\S+)/m);
   211	    if (!sc) {
   212	      const attempt = noFence.split('\n').some((l) => l.includes('source-commit:') && !/^source-commit:\s+\S+/.test(l));
   213	      notes.push(attempt
   214	        ? `${m}: source-commit is not in the canonical fence-outside line-start form 'source-commit: <ref>'`
   215	        : `${m}: truth doc has no source-commit`);

   217	    }
   218	    const ref = sc[1];
   219	    const explicit = entry.sourceFiles !== null;
   220	    const declared = explicit ? entry.sourceFiles : [`lib/${m}.js`];
   221	    // an explicit declaration is a complete promise: every token must be verifiable
   222	    const usable = [];
   223	    let tokenBad = null;
   224	    for (const rel of declared) {
   225	      // an explicit token is a repo-relative promise: reject absolute/backslash/empty/. or .. segments
   226	      if (explicit && (rel === '' || path.isAbsolute(rel) || rel.includes('\\') ||
   227	          rel.split('/').some((seg) => seg === '' || seg === '.' || seg === '..'))) {
   228	        tokenBad = `${m}: malformed source-files token (must be a normalized repo-relative path): ${rel}`; break;
   229	      }
   230	      const abs = path.join(cwd, rel);
   231	      let st = null; try { st = fs.lstatSync(abs); } catch { /* absent */ }
   232	      if (!st) { if (explicit) { tokenBad = `${m}: declared source-files token missing: ${rel}`; break; } continue; }
   233	      if (st.isSymbolicLink()) { if (explicit) { tokenBad = `${m}: declared source-files token is a symlink: ${rel}`; break; } continue; }
   234	      if (!containsReal(cwd, abs)) { if (explicit) { tokenBad = `${m}: declared source-files token escapes the repo: ${rel}`; break; } continue; }
   235	      if (!st.isFile() && !st.isDirectory()) { if (explicit) { tokenBad = `${m}: declared source-files token is neither file nor directory: ${rel}`; break; } continue; }
   236	      usable.push(rel);
   237	    }
   238	    if (tokenBad) { blocked.push(tokenBad); continue; }
   239	    if (!usable.length) { notes.push(`${m}: no ${declared.join(' / ')} to compare`); continue; }
   240	    const g = spawnSync('git', ['-C', cwd, 'log', '--oneline', `${ref}..HEAD`, '--', ...usable], { encoding: 'utf8' });
   241	    if (g.error) { notes.push(`${m}: git unavailable (${g.error.message})`); continue; }
   242	    if (g.status !== 0) { notes.push(`${m}: git failed (${(g.stderr || '').split('\n')[0]})`); continue; }
   243	    const n = g.stdout.trim() ? g.stdout.trim().split('\n').length : 0;
   244	    if (n > 0) blocked.push(`${m}: ${n} commit(s) since ${ref}`);
   245	    else checked++;
   246	  }
   247	  if (blocked.length) return { id: 'C6', status: 'blocked', detail: blocked.join('; ') };
   248	  if (checked > 0) return { id: 'C6', status: 'pass', detail: `${checked} module stamp(s) up to date${notes.length ? `; ${notes.join('; ')}` : ''}` };
   249	  return { id: 'C6', status: 'n/a', detail: notes.join('; ') };
   250	}

   252	function checkBinding(cwd, name, stage, testCmd, idPattern) {
   253	  const run = stage === 'in-flight'
   254	    ? verify({ change: name, cwd, testCmd, idPattern })
   255	    : verify({ specs: [path.join(cwd, 'apriori', 'specs')], cwd, testCmd, idPattern });
   256	  if (run.errors.length) return { infra: run.errors };
   257	  const v = run.verdict;
   258	  const clean = v.clean && run.duplicates.length === 0;
   259	  // in-flight runs carry the informative store summary (change-scoped verdict, GT-26/27)
   260	  const sr2 = run.storeReport;
   261	  const suffix = sr2
   262	    ? `; store: ${sr2.boundRed.length} red, ${sr2.unbound.length} unbound, ${sr2.orphan.length} orphan, ${sr2.unidentified.length} unidentified, ${sr2.unattributedFailures.count} unattributed, ${sr2.duplicates.length} duplicate(s) outstanding`
   263	    : '';
   264	  const label0 = sr2 ? `${stage}, change-scoped` : stage;
   265	  if (clean) return { check: { id: 'C1', status: 'pass', detail: `verify GREEN (${label0})${suffix}` }, projection: run.projection };
   266	  const parts = [];
   267	  for (const [label, arr] of [['red', v.boundRed], ['unbound', v.unbound], ['orphan', v.orphan], ['unidentified', v.unidentified], ['unattributed-failures', v.unattributed || []], ['duplicate-IDs', run.duplicates]])
   268	    if (arr.length) parts.push(`${arr.length} ${label}`);
   269	  return { check: { id: 'C1', status: 'blocked', detail: `verify GAPS: ${parts.join(', ')}${suffix}` }, projection: run.projection };
   270	}
   271	
   272	
   273	// C7: unstamped mutation deltas are denied by default — a waiver is always visible (GT-16).
   274	// Archived stage: the deltas are already merged; there is nothing left to stamp-check.
   275	function checkCas(projection, stage, noCas, cwd) {
   276	  if (stage === 'archived') return { id: 'C7', status: 'n/a', detail: 'deltas already merged' };
   277	  const um = (projection && projection.unstampedMutations) || [];
   278	  if (!um.length) return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'every mutation delta is stamped' };  // nothing to consult — silent pass (GT-16)
   279	  if (noCas) return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'waived (--no-cas)' };
   280	  const problem = configCasProblem(cwd);                       // consumption-time only
   281	  if (problem) return { id: 'C7', status: 'blocked', detail: `${problem} — a broken config never equals a waiver` };
   282	  if (configCas(cwd) === 'optional') return { id: 'C7', status: 'pass', detail: 'waived (process-config cas: optional)' };
   283	  return { id: 'C7', status: 'blocked',
   284	    detail: `unstamped mutation delta(s): ${um.join(', ')} — run: apriori stamp <store-file> (or waive with --no-cas / a process-config cas: optional row)` };
   285	}
   286	
   287	// The whole evaluation. → { code, stage, checks, result, blocked, errors }
   288	function runGate(opts) {
   289	  const cwd = opts.cwd || process.cwd();
   290	  const res = { code: 0, stage: null, checks: [], result: 'PASS', blocked: 0, errors: [], change: opts.change || null };
   291	  if (!opts.change) return err(res, USAGE);   // the ONE usage constant — every path lists the same flags
   292	  const nameCheck = require('./resolve').validateChangeName(opts.change);
   293	  if (!nameCheck.ok) return err(res, `invalid change name '${opts.change}' (${nameCheck.kind}) — bare kebab-case, not date-prefixed, not a reserved name`);
   294	  const loc = resolveChange(cwd, opts.change);
   295	  if (loc.error) return err(res, loc.error);
   296	  res.stage = loc.stage;
   297	  const flowPath = path.join(loc.dir, 'flow-state.md');
   298	  if (!fs.existsSync(flowPath)) return err(res, `no readable flow-state.md at ${flowPath} — tier-aware checks are impossible`);
   299	  let state;
   300	  try { state = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(flowPath, 'utf8')); }
   301	  catch (e) { return err(res, `flow-state unreadable: ${e.message}`); }
   302	  let testCmd = opts.testCmd;
   303	  if (!testCmd) {
   304	    const c = configTestCmd(cwd);
   305	    if (c && c.error) return err(res, `verify: ${c.error}`);
   306	    testCmd = c;
   307	  }
   308	  if (!testCmd) return err(res, 'no test command: pass --test-cmd or add a test-cmd row to apriori/process-config.md');
   309	
   310	  const b = checkBinding(cwd, opts.change, loc.stage, testCmd, opts.idPattern === undefined ? null : opts.idPattern);
   311	  if (b.infra) { for (const e of b.infra) res.errors.push(`verify: ${e}`); res.result = 'ERROR'; res.code = 2; return res; }
   312	  res.checks.push(b.check);
   313	  const tier = TIER_ENUM.includes(state.tier) ? state.tier : null;
   314	  res.checks.push(checkTasks(loc.dir, tier));
   315	  res.checks.push(checkFlowState(state, opts.change));
   316	  const rdDefect = reviewDirDefect(loc.dir);
   317	  if (rdDefect) {
   318	    res.checks.push({ id: 'C4', status: 'blocked', detail: rdDefect });
   319	    res.checks.push({ id: 'C5', status: 'blocked', detail: rdDefect });
   320	  } else {
   321	    res.checks.push(checkLedger(tier, loc.stage, loc.dir));
   322	    res.checks.push(checkEvidence(loc.dir));
   323	  }
   324	
   325	  res.checks.push(checkKb(cwd, loc.dir));
   326	  res.checks.push(checkCas(b.projection, loc.stage, !!opts.noCas, cwd));
   327	
   328	  res.blocked = res.checks.filter((c) => c.status === 'blocked').length;
   329	  res.result = res.blocked ? 'BLOCKED' : 'PASS';
   330	  res.code = res.blocked ? 1 : 0;
   331	  return res;
   332	}
   333	
   334	function toJson(res) {
   335	  return { change: res.change, stage: res.stage, checks: res.checks, result: res.result, blocked: res.blocked, errors: res.errors };
   336	}
   337	
   338	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori gate --change <name> [--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--id-pattern <re>] [--cwd <dir>] [--json] [--no-cas]';
   339	
   340	function cli(argv) {
   341	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'gate', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   342	    flags: { '--change': 'value', '--test-cmd': 'value', '--id-pattern': 'value', '--cwd': 'value', '--json': 'flag', '--no-cas': 'flag' },
   343	    jsonError: (m) => JSON.stringify({ change: null, stage: null, checks: [], result: 'ERROR', blocked: 0, errors: [m] }, null, 2) }, (f) => {
   344	    const a = { change: f['--change'] || null, testCmd: f['--test-cmd'] || null,
   345	      idPattern: ('--id-pattern' in f) ? f['--id-pattern'] : null,   // PRESENCE, not truthiness (GT-24 empty flag)
   346	      cwd: f['--cwd'] || process.cwd(), json: !!f['--json'], noCas: !!f['--no-cas'] };
   347	    const res = runGate(a);
   348	    if (a.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(toJson(res), null, 2)); return res.code; }
   349	    for (const e of res.errors) console.error('gate: ' + e);
   350	    const mark = { pass: '✓', blocked: '✗', 'n/a': '–' };
   351	    for (const c of res.checks) console.log(`${mark[c.status]} ${c.id} ${c.status === 'blocked' ? 'BLOCKED — ' : ''}${c.detail}`);
   352	    if (res.code !== 2) console.log(res.code === 0 ? `\nGATE: PASS — ${CAVEAT}` : `\nGATE: BLOCKED (${res.blocked} item(s))`);
   353	    return res.code;
   354	  });
   355	}
   356	
   357	module.exports = { runGate, resolveChange, classifyStatus, cli };
